<pasted_content id="39f2">
Fix knowledge-commons so projects don't believe they need graph-init to add a new entity type.

Problem: the plugin teaches that a graph's `.commons.yml` `entity:` list "belongs to graph-init" and that re-interviewing graph-init's block 2 is "the sanctioned way to change declared types." That's wrong in practice. Derek adds entity types by direct edit with his approval: financial-planning added `<type>` on <date> and `<type>` on <date> that way, with no re-interview. Generated graphs keep repeating the wrong route back to him.

Where the claim lives (pull first; the local checkout at ~/dev/claude-code-plugins is behind origin/main):
- plugins/knowledge-commons/references/templates/process.md, the "missing entity type" passage in the report section (around lines 426 and 445).
- plugins/knowledge-commons/references/deltas.md, the `step10-entity-type-gap` delta (version 0.5.0): its instruction (point d) and its satisfied-test both require "pointing at graph-init's block-2 re-interview."
- Search the rest of the plugin (graph-init/SKILL.md, the knowledge-graph template, graph-conventions.md, other deltas) for the same claim. airbnb-prep's generated knowledge-graph skill carries it too, so the knowledge-graph template may as well.

What the corrected behavior should say:
- A new entity type is a direct edit once the graph's owner says yes: an entry in `.commons.yml`'s `entity:` list, a directory, a map, an atlas link, a line in the generated knowledge-graph skill's type list (and the process skill's inspect-step mention), then backfill. A graph-init re-interview is optional, never required.
- Keep what's right: /process still only recommends during a run and never edits `.commons.yml` itself; the recurrence bar and the `[entity-type-gap: ...]` cadence stay; /graph-patch still doesn't own or change the `entity:` list.
- Where a graph has no `entity:` list yet, establishing the tier is still the larger ask and should be said plainly, but the route is the same direct edit.

Constraints:
- Follow the repo's CLAUDE.md and .claude/rules (template-deltas.md in particular). `step10-entity-type-gap` is already stamped as applied in generated graphs, so it needs a superseding delta, not an in-place edit. The new delta's satisfied-test must fail any section that still names graph-init as the required route.
- Don't edit the downstream generated graphs from this session. They pick the fix up through /graph-patch. Graphs known to carry the old text on devbox: airbnb-prep, daily-digest, persimmon-care, service-health-monitor, email-triggers, log-archaeologist. financial-planning was already fixed by hand (commit b149f72 in ddehart/financial-planning); use its wording as a reference if it helps.

Done looks like: template and any other plugin text corrected, a superseding delta with rationale and satisfied-test, a version bump per the repo's convention, and a PR. List the downstream graphs to run /graph-patch on after it merges.
</pasted_content id="39f2">

⏺ Bash(gh pr create --base main --title "fix(knowledge-commons): entity types are a direct edit, not a graph-init re-interview" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Problem

The `process` template's report step said `.commons.yml`'s `entity:` list "belongs to `graph-init`" and that re-interviewing its block 2 is "the sanctioned way to change declared types." That isn't how types get added. Owners add them by direct edit with their own approval: financial-planning (`<type>` <date>, `<type>` <date>), claude-usage (first three, <date>), osu-builder-in-residence (<date>, <date>), airbnb-prep (`tool`, <date>). The route was also incomplete. A re-interview rewrites `.commons.yml` but not the hand-sharpened generated skills, so their type lists go stale. airbnb-prep's said "none declared" for 17 days after its tier existed.

## Changes

- **`process` template:** the missing-entity-type passage now names a direct edit once the owner says yes. It lists the work: an `entity:` entry, a directory, a map, an atlas link, a line in the `knowledge-graph` skill's type list, the process skill's own mentions (such as the inspect step), and backfill. A re-interview is optional. These stay unchanged: recommend-only during the run, the recurrence bar, the `[entity-type-gap: ...]` cadence, and the "no `entity:` list means a larger ask" case.
- **`knowledge-graph` template:** the Entities entry says how a type is added and that this list needs the edit.
- **Deltas:**
  - `step10-entity-type-gap-direct-edit` supersedes point (d) of `step10-entity-type-gap`. The old entry is kept unchanged, with a comment, because graphs have it stamped. The new satisfied-test fails any section that still names graph-init as the required route or owner.
  - `kg-types-entity-route` reaches local copies of the claim in `knowledge-graph` skills (airbnb-prep's has one).
- **`/graph-patch`: an anchor can now be a list of exact headings** (spec D23). Exactly one must match, otherwise the delta skips as missing or ambiguous, as before. This was needed because 0.6.0 split the live graphs between `## 10. Report` (patched) and `## 12. Report` (generated at 0.6.0). A single anchor would skip in about half of them, and that skip would come back on every run. Any future delta targeting a stage after Resolve hits the same split. The delta log, the template-deltas rule, and the README are updated to match.
- **Version 0.6.0 → 0.7.0.** This is minor because anchor lists are new behavior. Note: `docs/specs/knowledge-commons-question-graduation.md` (R18) also plans 0.7.0, so whichever of the two merges second takes 0.8.0.

[redacted: the per-graph patch list, removed from the published PR body at Derek's direction]

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)")

## Problem

The `process` template's report step said `.commons.yml`'s `entity:` list "belongs to `graph-init`" and that re-interviewing its block 2 is "the sanctioned way to change declared types." In practice, owners add entity types by direct edit with their own approval, without a re-interview. The route was also incomplete. A re-interview rewrites `.commons.yml` but not the hand-sharpened generated skills, so their type lists go stale.

## Changes

- **`process` template:** the missing-entity-type passage now names a direct edit once the owner says yes. It lists the work: an `entity:` entry, a directory, a map, an atlas link, a line in the `knowledge-graph` skill's type list, the process skill's own mentions of the types, and backfill. A re-interview is optional. These stay unchanged: recommend-only during the run, the recurrence bar, the `[entity-type-gap: ...]` cadence, and the "no `entity:` list means a larger ask" case.
- **`knowledge-graph` template:** the Entities entry says how a type is added and that this list needs the edit.
- **Deltas:**
  - `step10-entity-type-gap-direct-edit` supersedes point (d) of `step10-entity-type-gap`. The old entry is kept unchanged, with a comment, because graphs have it stamped. The new satisfied-test fails any section that still names graph-init as the required route or owner.
  - `kg-types-entity-route` reaches local copies of the claim in `knowledge-graph` skills.
- **`/graph-patch`: an anchor can now be a list of exact headings** (spec D23). Exactly one must match, otherwise the delta skips as missing or ambiguous, as before. This was needed because 0.6.0 split live graphs between `## 10. Report` (patched) and `## 12. Report` (generated at 0.6.0). A single anchor would skip in about half of them on every run. The delta log, the template-deltas rule, and the README are updated to match.
- **Version 0.6.0 → 0.7.0.** This is minor because anchor lists are new behavior. The question-graduation spec (R18) also plans 0.7.0, so whichever of the two merges second takes 0.8.0.

Run `/graph-patch` in each downstream graph that has `step10-entity-type-gap` applied. Graphs already fixed by hand should report the process delta as already satisfied.

⏺ PR [ddehart/claude-code-plugins#78](https://github.com/ddehart/claude-code-plugins/pull/78) is open. It isn't merged yet, and the repo has no CI checks.

**What changed**
- **process template:** the missing-entity-type passage now says a new type is a direct edit once the owner says yes. It lists the work: the `entity:` entry, a directory, a map, an atlas link, a line in the knowledge-graph skill's type list, the process skill's own mentions of the types, and backfill. A graph-init re-interview is optional. The template now says why the skill edits are needed either way: a re-interview doesn't rewrite the generated skills. Everything else in the passage stays as it was: /process only recommends during a run, plus the recurrence bar, the `[entity-type-gap: ...]` cadence, and saying plainly that a graph with no `entity:` list has the larger ask.
- **knowledge-graph template:** it never made the claim, but airbnb-prep's copy picked it up locally. The Entities entry now says how a type is added.
- **Deltas:**
  - `step10-entity-type-gap-direct-edit` replaces the old delta's route. Its satisfied-test fails any section that still says graph-init is the required route or owns the list. Mentioning a re-interview as optional, or as a past claim that was corrected, still passes.
  - `kg-types-entity-route` fixes the same claim in knowledge-graph skills.
  - The old delta keeps its text, with a comment pointing to the new one.

**One thing beyond the brief (you picked it when I asked):** /graph-patch now accepts an anchor that's a list of exact headings, and exactly one must match. The graphs that need this fix are split: some title the section `## 10. Report` (graphs patched up to 0.6.0), others `## 12. Report` (graphs generated at 0.6.0). With a single heading, the delta would skip in about half of them on every run. The patch-mechanism spec has this as D23. Because this is new behavior, the version went to 0.7.0, not 0.6.1. The question-graduation spec also plans 0.7.0, and there's an empty worktree for it, so whichever lands second needs 0.8.0.

**I loosened one test clause:** the new satisfied-test doesn't require a section to cover the "no `entity:` list" case if its graph already declares types. Otherwise /graph-patch would push that paragraph back into financial-planning and claude-usage, which dropped it on purpose.

**After merge, run /graph-patch in these.** I checked each one: all have the old delta stamped and still name the graph-init route. You left the last four off your list.
- airbnb-prep (process and knowledge-graph)
- daily-digest
- persimmon-care
- service-health-monitor
- email-triggers
- log-archaeologist
- claude-usage (its route is already a direct edit, but it still says the list "belongs to graph-init")
- daily-briefing
- devbox
- wellstead
- claude-code-plugins (this repo's own graph)

financial-planning and osu-builder-in-residence should show the process fix as already satisfied and only offer the one-sentence knowledge-graph addition. I matched the new tests against their text by reading it; I haven't run /graph-patch there.

I trimmed the PR description as you asked, so this list appears only here.

⏺ I found 7 issues in PR 78; nothing is fixed yet.

The most serious: the new process delta's anchor lists only `## 10. Report` and `## 12. Report`. A graph generated at 0.6.0 without a synthesis stage drops that section and renumbers, so it would title the report `## 11. Report`. In such a graph the delta would skip on every run, and the old route claim would never get corrected. None of the graphs on devbox has that heading, but the fix is just adding the third heading to the list.

Next in importance:
- **The old delta's replacement is noted only in a YAML comment**, and /graph-patch isn't told to read comments. A graph that never applied the old delta would be offered the wrong route, then offered its removal right after. Fixing it properly means teaching /graph-patch to recognize a replaced delta.
- **graph-init still offers a re-interview when run on an existing graph**, with no note that adding a type is a direct edit. Someone who runs it to add a type would end up with stale type lists in the generated skills.

The rest are small:
- stale counts in the delta log's note on numbering
- the patch-mechanism spec's header still saying v0.5.0 and "D1–D22"
- template wording that says a re-interview "is not the path to name" right before calling it an option
- one long unwrapped line in /graph-patch

> Fix all

**A superseded delta is applied as its successor amends it.** A delta may carry an optional
`supersedes:` field naming an earlier delta id that it corrects in part. Whenever that earlier delta is
pending, whether alone, by argument, or in the same run as its successor, apply it with the successor's
`instruction` taking precedence wherever the two conflict. Don't write content the successor removes,
and answer the earlier delta's `satisfied-test` without the clauses the successor replaces. Never apply
the superseded content and then propose removing it in the next delta: the reader would approve two
contradictory edits, and a skip on the second would leave the wrong one standing. Each delta is still
stamped only as it verifies. When the earlier delta is already applied, the successor is an ordinary
pending delta.
'''),

⏺ Bash(cd /home/derek/dev/claude-code-plugins/plugins/knowledge-commons && python3 - <<'EOF'
import pathlib,textwrap,re
def reflow(path, start_marker, end_marker, width=104, indent_cont=""):
    p=pathlib.Path(path); t=p.read_text()
    i=t.index(start_marker); j=t.index(end_marker,i)+len(end_marker)
    para=t[i:j]; words=" ".join(l.strip() for l in para.split("\n"))
    lines=textwrap.wrap(words,width=width,subsequent_indent=indent_cont,break_on_hyphens=False,break_long_words=False)
    p.write_text(t[:i]+"\n".join(lines)+t[j:])
reflow("skills/graph-patch/SKILL.md","Each log entry carries seven fields","starting the next.",100)
reflow("skills/graph-patch/SKILL.md","Find the `anchor` as an **exact heading match**","not a pattern.",100)
reflow("skills/graph-patch/SKILL.md","- **Not found → skip it, loudly.**","rather than landing somewhere convincing.",100,"  ")
reflow("references/templates/process.md","Then point at the work instead of doing any of it. Where this graph already","does not create the directory or the\ntier.",104)
EOF

⏺ I fixed all 7 review findings and pushed them to [PR 78](https://github.com/ddehart/claude-code-plugins/pull/78) as a second commit (a879a91). The delta log still reads cleanly: 14 entries, all with their required fields, and the one `supersedes` link points back to the delta it replaces. The PR isn't merged.

- **Missing heading:** the new delta's anchor list now includes `## 11. Report`, the heading a 0.6.0 graph without a synthesis stage would have. The /graph-patch example, the delta log's numbering note, and the spec's reasoning all mention it now.
- **Replacing an old delta:** a delta can now carry an optional `supersedes:` field (spec D24). Where the old delta hasn't been applied yet, /graph-patch now applies it with the newer delta's correction already in place. It no longer offers the wrong text first and its removal right after. The new delta uses the field. The comment on the old entry now just points to it, and the delta-log field table, the template-deltas rule, and the spec cover it.
- **graph-init:** when it's run on an existing graph and you want a new entity type, it now offers the direct edit and lists the files involved. It says why a re-interview isn't enough: that would update `.commons.yml` but leave the skills' type lists stale.
- **Template wording:** the process template now just says a re-interview is "optional, never required", matching what the delta's test accepts.
- **Smaller fixes:** the stale "twelve anchors" count in the delta log is corrected, and the spec header now records D23 and D24. Three overlong paragraphs in /graph-patch and the process template are rewrapped.

Version stays at 0.7.0, since none of this has shipped. After the merge, run /graph-patch in the same graphs I listed earlier.

⏺ Bash(python3 - <<'EOF'
import pathlib,re
def rep(path, pairs):
    p=pathlib.Path(path); t=p.read_text()
    for a,b in pairs:
        assert t.count(a)==1,(path,a[:70],t.count(a)); t=t.replace(a,b)
    p.write_text(t)
K="plugins/knowledge-commons/"
D=K+"references/deltas.md"

# --- rewrite the successor entry as a self-contained end state
p=pathlib.Path(D); t=p.read_text()
i=t.index("- id: step10-entity-type-gap-direct-edit"); j=t.index("- id: kg-types-entity-route")
new_entry='''- id: step10-entity-type-gap-direct-edit
  file: process
  anchor: ["## 10. Report", "## 11. Report", "## 12. Report"]
  version: 0.7.0
  supersedes: step10-entity-type-gap
  instruction: >
    Have the run report recommend a new entity *type* (a new entry in .commons.yml's entity: list)
    when the run keeps naming a category of thing the graph has no declared type for. This is the end
    state step10-entity-type-gap described, with its route corrected, so a graph that applied that
    delta needs only the route changed. (a) It is NOT conditional on the graph already declaring an
    entity type: a graph whose interview answered "none" is the one that may need its first. (b) The
    bar is recurrence: several distinct instances of one category, named as things a reader would
    look up, none covered by a declared type; existing notes may corroborate where one run is thin.
    (c) The report names the category and the instances evidencing it. (d) The route is a direct
    edit, made once the graph's owner says yes: a new entry in the entity: list, a directory, a map,
    an atlas link, a line in the sibling knowledge-graph skill's type list, a mention wherever this
    process skill names the declared types (an inspect-step signal class, for example), and backfill.
    Remove any statement that the entity: list or .commons.yml's type declarations belong to
    graph-init or are graph-init's to write, and any statement that re-interviewing graph-init (its
    block 2, or its types questions) is the sanctioned, required, or only route. A re-interview may be
    named as optional, never required; if it is named, say it doesn't rewrite already-generated
    skills, so the skill edits are needed either way. Where the section covers a graph with no
    entity: list, say plainly that establishing the tier is the larger ask, by the same route; a
    section already rewritten for a graph that declares types may leave that case out. (e)
    Recommending is not doing: during the run the step never edits .commons.yml, adds the entry, or
    creates the directory; it may offer to make the edit after the run. Don't name /graph-patch as an
    owner of the entity: list; it amends only the generated: block and would correctly refuse. (f)
    Cadence: when raising a category, write a marker of the form [entity-type-gap: <category>] into
    the changelog entry this step already writes. Before raising one, scan the live changelog and the
    most recent monthly archive for the literal prefix, read the categories recorded under it, and
    stay quiet if one is the same category by meaning rather than by string equality. Nothing
    constrains how runs word a category, so a whole-marker match would re-raise the same gap every
    run. Suppression is per-category and expires when the marker rotates out of both files.
  rationale: >
    The graph-init route was never how types were actually added. Graph owners add entity types by
    direct edit with their own approval: financial-planning added <type> on <date> and
    <type> on <date> that way; claude-usage added its first three types on <date>;
    osu-builder-in-residence added program on <date> and published copy on <date>;
    airbnb-prep declared tool on <date> directly at the user's instruction. None of those
    involved a re-interview. Because generated skills repeated the graph-init route, they kept
    steering their owner toward a heavier path. The route was also incomplete. A re-interview
    rewrites .commons.yml, but graph-init won't overwrite hand-sharpened generated skills without
    asking. So a graph that followed it got a config declaring the new type and skills that still
    enumerated the old ones: airbnb-prep's knowledge-graph skill said "none declared" for seventeen
    days after its tier existed. The enumeration in the two generated skills is the part both routes
    kept missing, which is why (d) lists it. Points (a)-(c), (e) and (f) are step10-entity-type-gap's,
    unchanged; they are restated so this entry is complete on its own and the superseded one never
    needs applying.
  satisfied-test: >
    Does the section have the run report recommend adding a new entity TYPE to .commons.yml (not
    notes for individual nouns of an existing type) when the run repeatedly names a category no
    declared type covers, unconditionally on the graph already having an entity type, with a
    recurrence bar over multiple distinct instances, naming the category and its instances? Does it
    name a direct edit, made once the owner approves, as the route, listing among the work at least
    the entity: entry, a directory, a map, an atlas link, and the line in the sibling knowledge-graph
    skill's type list? Does it stop at recommending during the run, without editing .commons.yml?
    Does its cadence fix only the marker prefix [entity-type-gap:, scan both the live and the archived
    changelog, and judge whether a category was already raised by meaning? The section FAILS if, as a
    current instruction, it says the entity: list or type declarations belong to graph-init or are
    graph-init's to write; names a graph-init re-interview as the sanctioned, required, or only
    route; says /graph-patch owns or may change the entity: list; applies the check only when an
    entity tier exists; or matches the whole marker literally. Naming the re-interview as an optional
    alternative passes, and so does a sentence reporting the graph-init route as a former claim that
    was corrected. If the section covers a graph with no entity: list, it must call establishing the
    tier the larger ask, by the same route; a section for a graph that already declares types may
    omit that case.

'''
t=t[:i]+new_entry+t[j:]
p.write_text(t)

rep(G,[
('''**A superseded delta is applied as its successor amends it.** A delta may carry an optional
`supersedes:` field naming an earlier delta id that it corrects in part. Whenever that earlier delta is
pending, whether alone, by argument, or in the same run as its successor, apply it with the successor's
`instruction` taking precedence wherever the two conflict. Don't write content the successor removes,
and answer the earlier delta's `satisfied-test` without the clauses the successor replaces. Never apply
the superseded content and then propose removing it in the next delta: the reader would approve two
contradictory edits, and a skip on the second would leave the wrong one standing. Each delta is still
stamped only as it verifies. When the earlier delta is already applied, the successor is an ordinary
pending delta.''','''**A superseded delta is replaced, not applied.** A delta may carry an optional `supersedes:` naming
an earlier delta id. The successor states the full end state with its own complete `satisfied-test`,
so while the earlier id is pending, don't select it: applying it would propose content the successor
then removes. Stamp it in step 5 alongside its successor, once the successor verifies or is found
already satisfied; if the successor is skipped or fails, both stay pending. Follow a chain the same way
(a superseded successor hands its predecessors to its own successor). If an argument names a superseded
id, say which delta replaces it and apply that one instead.'''),
('''Find the `anchor` as an **exact heading match** in the target file. `anchor` is either one heading

a graph patched up to 0.6.0 kept `## 10. Report`). For a list, look for every entry; the anchor
resolves only if the list yields **exactly one** heading, once, in this file. Each entry is matched
exactly, the same as a string anchor. A list is a set of exact alternatives, not a pattern.''','''Find the `anchor` as an **exact heading match** in the target file. `anchor` is either one heading

anchor resolves only if the list yields **exactly one** heading, once, in this file. A list is a set of
exact alternatives, not a pattern.'''),

⏺ I applied the cleanup and pushed it to PR 78 as commit bd7a649. The delta log still parses: 14 entries, and the one replaced delta is linked to its successor.

**Fixed**
- **Replacing an old delta is simpler now.** Two reviewers pointed out that my earlier fix made /graph-patch blend two instructions together and invent its own pass/fail test while running. Now the new delta states the whole corrected section and carries its own complete test. /graph-patch never applies the old delta, and marks it applied once the new one passes. A graph that already has the old delta only gets an edit to the part that changed. I updated the spec, the field table, the repo rule, and /graph-patch's stamping step to match.
- **The new delta stands on its own.** It restates the parts of the old delta that were right. Its reasoning field no longer explains the log format, since the numbering note and the spec already cover that.
- **Duplicated text is gone.**
  - /graph-patch states the heading-list rule without listing the current headings.
  - The spec's explanation of anchor lists points at the delta log's note on numbering instead of repeating it, and the sentence I had spliced in mid-paragraph is fixed.
  - The comment above the old delta is two lines.
  - graph-init points at the template's list of work instead of copying it.
- **The knowledge-graph skill gets one sentence, not a paragraph.** That skill is read on every search and capture, so its Entities entry now says only: direct edit, a re-interview is never required, add a line to this list, and the process skill's report step has the rest. I trimmed the matching delta to fit.

**Skipped**
- **Dropping the re-interview sentence from the process template.** The efficiency reviewer called it dead weight. I kept it because you asked for the skills to say a re-interview is optional, never required.
- **One approval for a pair of related deltas, and making the knowledge-graph skill read types straight from `.commons.yml`.** Both are bigger changes to how the mechanism works than this PR should carry.
- **Matching anchors on the section title with the number removed.** This was the altitude reviewer's top finding, and it's a real alternative to the heading lists you picked. Template section titles are unique, so it would stay an exact match. No delta would ever need to list every numbering, and future renumbering wouldn't matter. It means rewriting the anchor rule I just added.

> Yes, we can switch. You'll also need to fix the merge conflicts, please.

import re,sys,collections
L=[l.rstrip("\n") for l in open(sys.argv[1]) if l.startswith("## ")]

c=collections.Counter(T); d=[k for k,v in c.items() if v>1]
print(sys.argv[1].split("/")[-4], "report:",c["## Report"], "dups:",d)
EOF

⏺ Bash(gh pr edit 78 --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Problem

The `process` template's report step said `.commons.yml`'s `entity:` list "belongs to `graph-init`" and that re-interviewing its block 2 is "the sanctioned way to change declared types." In practice, owners add entity types by direct edit with their own approval, without a re-interview. The route was also incomplete. A re-interview rewrites `.commons.yml` but not the hand-sharpened generated skills, so their type lists go stale.

## Changes

- **`process` template:** the missing-entity-type passage names a direct edit once the owner says yes. It lists the work: an `entity:` entry, a directory, a map, an atlas link, a line in the `knowledge-graph` skill's type list, the process skill's own mentions of the types, and backfill. A re-interview is optional, never required. These stay unchanged: recommend-only during the run, the recurrence bar, the `[entity-type-gap: ...]` cadence, and the "no `entity:` list means a larger ask" case.
- **`knowledge-graph` template:** the Entities entry says, in one clause, that a new type is a direct edit that includes a line in this list.
- **graph-init:** when the user wants a new entity type on an existing graph, it offers the direct edit instead of a re-interview.
- **Deltas:**
  - `step10-entity-type-gap-direct-edit` replaces `step10-entity-type-gap`. It states the full corrected end state and has its own complete satisfied-test. That test fails any section still naming graph-init as the required route or owner. The old entry is kept unchanged, with a comment, because graphs have it stamped.
  - `kg-types-entity-route` reaches local copies of the claim in `knowledge-graph` skills.
- **`/graph-patch`, two mechanism changes:**
  - *Title-matched `process` anchors* (spec D23). The patcher drops the generated `## <N>. ` stage number and compares titles exactly. 0.6.0 left live graphs at `## 10. Report` (patched), `## 12. Report` (fresh), or `## 11. Report` (fresh, no synthesis tier). One anchor now resolves in all three and survives later renumbering. Checked against 13 live graphs: "Report" resolves once in each, with no duplicate titles.
  - *Optional `supersedes` field* (spec D24). A superseded delta is never applied while pending. It is stamped when its complete successor verifies, so no graph is offered wrong content followed by its removal.
- **Version 0.8.0.** Question graduation took 0.7.0 on main; main is merged into this branch.

## After merge

Run `/graph-patch` in each downstream graph that has `step10-entity-type-gap` applied. Graphs already fixed by hand should report the process delta as already satisfied.

import re,pathlib,os
roots={"airbnb-prep":"~/projects/airbnb-prep","daily-digest":"~/projects/daily-digest","persimmon-care":"~/projects/persimmon-care","service-health-monitor":"~/projects/service-health-monitor","email-triggers":"~/projects/email-triggers","log-archaeologist":"~/projects/log-archaeologist","daily-briefing":"~/projects/daily-briefing","claude-usage":"~/projects/claude-usage","devbox":"~/devbox","wellstead":"~/dev/wellstead","claude-code-plugins":"~/dev/claude-code-plugins","financial-planning":"~/projects/financial-planning","osu-builder-in-residence":"~/dev/osu-builder-in-residence"}
for n,r in roots.items():
    t=open(n+".md").read()
    secs=re.split(r"^## ",t,flags=re.M)[1:]
    for s in secs:
        head=s.splitlines()[0]
        f="knowledge-graph" if "(knowledge-graph)" in head else "process"
        src=open(os.path.expanduser(r)+f"/.claude/skills/{f}/SKILL.md").read()
        befores=re.findall(r"### Before[^\n]*\n```[^\n]*\n(.*?)\n```",s,flags=re.S)
        res=[src.count(b) for b in befores]
        print(f"{n:26} {head[:55]:55} befores={res}")
EOF)

import pathlib
p=pathlib.Path("plugins/knowledge-commons/references/deltas.md"); t=p.read_text()
pairs=[
('''    creates the directory; it may offer to make the edit after the run. Don't name /graph-patch as an
    owner of the entity: list; it amends only the generated: block and would correctly refuse. (f)
    Cadence: when raising a category, write a marker of the form [entity-type-gap: <category>] into
    the changelog entry this step already writes. Before raising one, scan the live changelog and the
    most recent monthly archive for the literal prefix, read the categories recorded under it, and
    stay quiet if one is the same category by meaning rather than by string equality. Nothing
    constrains how runs word a category, so a whole-marker match would re-raise the same gap every
    run. Suppression is per-category and expires when the marker rotates out of both files.''',
'''    creates the directory; it may offer to make the edit after the run. Don't name /graph-patch as an
    owner of the entity: list; it amends only the generated: block and would correctly refuse. (f)
    Cadence: a category already raised is not raised again while its record lasts, and sameness is
    judged by meaning rather than by string equality, because nothing constrains how runs word a
    category. The template does this with a marker of the form [entity-type-gap: <category>] in the
    changelog entry this step already writes, scanning the live changelog and the most recent monthly
    archive for its fixed prefix. A graph that already keeps that record another way (a marker
    without brackets, a shadow log) keeps its own mechanism: never change an existing graph's marker
    form, since its recorded markers would stop matching and every category already raised would be
    raised again.'''),
('''    Points (a)-(c), (e) and (f) are step10-entity-type-gap's,
    unchanged; they are restated so this entry is complete on its own and the superseded one never
    needs applying.''','''    Points (a)-(c), (e) and (f) are step10-entity-type-gap's,
    unchanged; they are restated so this entry is complete on its own and the superseded one never
    needs applying. Point (f) and its test clause are stated by substance rather than by the
    template's literal marker: the first patch run (2026-11-08) found four graphs that had already
    kept the cadence a different way on purpose, three with unbracketed markers and one with a
    shadow log, and a literal clause would have left their edit permanently unverifiable. That
    correction was made in place, before any graph had stamped this id.'''),
('''    skill's type list? Does it stop at recommending during the run, without editing .commons.yml?
    Does its cadence fix only the marker prefix [entity-type-gap:, scan both the live and the archived
    changelog, and judge whether a category was already raised by meaning? The section FAILS if, as a
    current instruction, it says the entity: list or type declarations belong to graph-init or are
    graph-init's to write; names a graph-init re-interview as the sanctioned, required, or only
    route; says /graph-patch owns or may change the entity: list; applies the check only when an
    entity tier exists; or matches the whole marker literally.''','''    skill's type list? Does it stop at recommending during the run, without editing .commons.yml?
    Does it have a cadence that keeps an already-raised category from being raised again, with a
    record that survives the changelog's monthly rotation and a sameness judgment made by meaning?
    Any fixed record form passes: the template's [entity-type-gap: prefix, the same prefix without
    brackets, or a graph's own log. The section FAILS if, as a current instruction, it says the
    entity: list or type declarations belong to graph-init or are graph-init's to write; names a
    graph-init re-interview as the sanctioned, required, or only route; says /graph-patch owns or
    may change the entity: list; applies the check only when an entity tier exists; or decides
    sameness by matching a free-form category name literally.'''),
]

EOF

fix(knowledge-commons): state the entity-type cadence test by substance

step10-entity-type-gap-direct-edit restated the old delta's cadence and its

run found four graphs that keep the cadence another way on purpose (three

EOF

# graph-patch review: 13 graphs, 25 edits

Each section below is one project's proposal: the exact text that would be replaced (Before) and the replacement (After). Every Before block has been checked to appear exactly once in its file. Nothing has been written to any project yet.

**Already satisfied, no edit:** financial-planning's process skill.

**Needs your call:**

- **daily-briefing, process skill.** Its local prose allows "direct in-run instantiation" of a new type on its own separate `y`. The edit moves that to an offer made after the run, per "the step only recommends during a run." Skip it and the delta stays pending on every later /graph-patch run.
- **claude-code-plugins, process skill.** The edit adds one local sentence: `plugin` and `skill` share `entities/` and `maps/entities.md`, so a new type may join them there.
- **devbox, wellstead, osu-builder-in-residence, airbnb-prep.** Their cadence records are kept as they are (unbracketed markers, and airbnb-prep's shadow log). That only verifies under the corrected test in PR #80.

**Noticed, not part of any edit (possible follow-ups):**

- persimmon-care hard-codes the plugin path `knowledge-commons/0.6.0` in both skills.
- daily-briefing's Report step lists its declared types without `job`.
- osu-builder-in-residence's "Unnamed parties and deliverables" signal class leaves out `program` and `published copy`.
- airbnb-prep's `.commons.yml` has a comment still saying types come from a block-2 re-interview. /graph-patch can't edit `.commons.yml` comments.

import yaml,glob,os,subprocess
t=open(os.path.expanduser("~/.claude/plugins/cache/ddehart-plugins/knowledge-commons/0.9.0/references/deltas.md")).read()
b=t.split('## Entries',1)[1].split('```yaml',1)[1].split('```',1)[0]
D=yaml.safe_load(b); print(len(D),"deltas; 0.9.0 ones:",[(d['id'],d['file']) for d in D if str(d['version'])>='0.9'])
sup={d['supersedes'] for d in D if d.get('supersedes')}
for c in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.expanduser("~/devbox/.commons.yml"))+glob.glob(os.path.expanduser("~/projects/*/.commons.yml"))+glob.glob(os.path.expanduser("~/dev/*/.commons.yml"))+glob.glob(os.path.expanduser("~/services/*/.commons.yml"))):
    p=os.path.dirname(c); y=yaml.safe_load(open(c)) or {}
    g=y.get('generated') or {}
    out=[]
    for f in ['process','knowledge-graph']:
        if not os.path.exists(f"{p}/.claude/skills/{f}/SKILL.md"): continue
        ap=set((g.get(f) or {}).get('applied') or [])
        pend=[d['id'] for d in D if d['file']==f and d['id'] not in ap and d['id'] not in sup]
        out.append(f"{f}:{pend if g else 'NO generated: block'}")
    dirty=subprocess.run(["git","-C",p,"status","--porcelain","--",".claude",".commons.yml"],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout.count("\n")
    print(os.path.basename(p), "dirty=%d"%dirty, " | ".join(out) or "no generated skills")
EOF)

⏺ Bash(cd /tmp/claude-1000/-home-derek-dev-claude-code-plugins/df62349c-9b7a-4eb5-a233-d94d2dd67c47/scratchpad/patch090 && python3 - <<'EOF'
import re,os
roots={"airbnb-prep":"~/projects/airbnb-prep","daily-digest":"~/projects/daily-digest","persimmon-care":"~/projects/persimmon-care","service-health-monitor":"~/projects/service-health-monitor","email-triggers":"~/projects/email-triggers","log-archaeologist":"~/projects/log-archaeologist","daily-briefing":"~/projects/daily-briefing","claude-usage":"~/projects/claude-usage","devbox":"~/devbox","wellstead":"~/dev/wellstead","claude-code-plugins":"~/dev/claude-code-plugins","financial-planning":"~/projects/financial-planning","osu-builder-in-residence":"~/dev/osu-builder-in-residence"}
tot=0
for n,r in roots.items():
    t=open(n+".md").read()
    for s in re.split(r"^## ",t,flags=re.M)[1:]:
        head=s.splitlines()[0]
        if "(process)" not in head and "(knowledge-graph)" not in head: continue
        f="knowledge-graph" if "(knowledge-graph)" in head else "process"
        src=open(os.path.expanduser(r)+f"/.claude/skills/{f}/SKILL.md").read()
        b=re.findall(r"### Before[^\n]*\n```[^\n]*\n(.*?)\n```",s,flags=re.S)
        st=re.search(r"Status:\s*\**\s*([a-z-]+)",s); st=st.group(1) if st else "?"
        tot+=len(b)
        print(f"{n:25} {f:16} {st:18} befores={[src.count(x) for x in b]}")
print("total Before blocks:",tot)
EOF)
